The dining room glowed beneath warm lanterns, which seemed as though they were purposely placed, leaving each table feeling intimate and secluded despite the room’s smaller size.  Guests sat beneath the glow dressed in tailored suits and evening gowns, their conversations quiet enough that even the clink of silverware against porcelain was louder than their murmurs.

Caitlyn found herself admiring the details of the elaborately decorated room, while the redolent scent of wine filled her nose.  Vi pulled her along gently by their conjoined hands, while Pierre pointed out a woven tapestry hanging on the wall, which displayed a serene, mountainous landscape.

“This was originally from Oxford, though it has hung here at the Inn for decades.” He said.

Above them, the tray ceiling was painted in a black and red floral pattern that was reminiscent of the old Italian villas the older woman had visited once during one of her summers off from undergrad.

It didn’t take long for her to notice the eyes around the room lingering on them as they followed the host.  Caitlyn’s heels thudded softly against the floor, which was covered in wall-to-wall Turkish and oriental rugs. 

“Violet, they’re staring,” she whispered from beneath the mask of her smile.

Vi glanced around the room, the patrons’ gazes flicking back to their plates once they knew they’d been caught.  She squeezed Caitlyn’s hand once, a new habit they both found they were quite fond of, “Let them. They’ve never been graced by the presence of a princess, you know.”

Caitlyn chuckled and rolled her eyes, though she gripped Vi’s hand tighter to show that she appreciated the younger woman’s levity in a moment where she felt slightly uncomfortable.  That was the thing about Vi.  She could lean on her in any situation and find comfort.

To her surprise, Pierre didn’t seat them at any of the tables in the main dining room.  Instead, he led them through the narrow hallway that led to the kitchen.

Stepping into the kitchen was like entering a whole new world for Caitlyn.  Sure, she’d dined at Michelin-starred restaurants before, but nothing could’ve prepared her for the pure artistry and theater she was about to experience.

Pots sizzled, knives thudded against cutting boards, pans clanged and steam whooshed as they entered the large space.  Chefs and sous chefs moved like actors in a play, sliding past each other in smooth, choreographed movements as they prepared plate after plate.

When their presence was eventually noticed, the entire line stopped what they were doing to cheer Vi’s name, something so heartwarming that it almost brought tears to Caitlyn’s eyes.  She looked over at the younger woman, finding her grinning ear to ear and offering a bashful wave.

Okay, she really needed to know how Vi was so connected to this place.

Pierre led them around the large, red-baked enamel range that served as the stark centerpiece of the room, where all the magic happened.  Above it hung the grandest copper range hood she’d ever seen, working hard to suck all the deliciously fragrant smoke and steam out of the room.

In the corner of the kitchen, nestled on either side of a baronial fireplace, were two kitchen tables.  The same server they’d seen just a few minutes ago at the host stand was just finishing their place settings as they walked up.

“Mademoiselle,” he greeted Caitlyn with a soft smile, gesturing for her to slide into the wooden banquette.

“Violet, I—” Caitlyn shook her head in complete awe and disbelief, her cerulean eyes dropped to the table as she reached for her cloth napkin, unfolding it and placing it in her lap with deft hands, smoothing the delicate fabric while she composed herself.  Then, when she looked back up, she found grey eyes waiting, “I’m speechless.”

“I know it all seems a bit…over the top,” Vi replied, shrugging off the compliment.  She reached for her water glass and took a sip.  Anything to distract from those cerulean eyes boring holes through her soul.

“Not at all. I mean, it’s unexpected, if I’m being honest.” Caitlyn replied gently.

That made Vi chuckle, “I know. Not exactly the type to be able to afford a three-star meal.”

“I didn’t mean—well, I—I don’t know what I meant, but—”

The younger woman held up a hand, stopping Caitlyn in her tracks, “I’m not offended, Cait. I really can’t afford this place.”

Confused, the older woman looked at her with furrowed brows, “Then…how are we…”

Vi sighed, unable to stall this conversation any longer.  She may as well just tell Caitlyn the truth and get it over with, “The owner was one of the rafters that my parents were able to save that day.”

The confession was brief, said with a certain surgical-like sterility that told Caitlyn she wasn’t ready to have a long, drawn-out conversation about it.

“Oh,” the older woman muttered, then smiled softly, reaching a hand over the table’s surface to find Vi’s own, “Well, then I’m even more overwhelmed with gratitude that you’ve brought me here, darling.”

Vi softened, letting go of the tension that’d gathered in the muscles of her shoulders, “On that note, they have an extensive wine list. Red or white, princess?”

The warm glow of the dining nook went hazy from the activity in the kitchen, the peak dinner rush in full bloom as clanging pans echoed across the blue-and-white ornate tiled walls.  Caramelized onion and toasted fennel seed wafted toward their table, making Vi’s stomach rumble as the two women spoke softly beneath the culinary din.

Caitlyn giggled, “Someone’s hungry.”

Vi groaned, “It’s Pierre’s fault, he always insists I have a full glass of wine before even bothering to take my food order.”

The older woman’s angular face looked sharper in the firelight, and her eyes twinkled with amusement, “I could wave him down, you know.”

“Well, I do like a woman that takes charge,” Vi replied with a quirk of her brow.

Caitlyn raised a delicate hand, catching Pierre’s attention from across the kitchen in less than ten seconds. 

“Yes, Mademoiselle?” He asked when he reached their table.

“I believe my handsome date and I are ready to order our courses,” her posh accent was smooth as velvet, “and I’d like another glass of wine, if it wouldn’t be too much to ask.”

“Yes, of course, would you like to look at our selection again?”

Caitlyn twirled the last sip of wine in her glass before looking back up at him, “No, that won’t be necessary, thank you.  We’ll have a couple glasses of the Bonneau du Martray, and as for entrée’s, my darling here will have the Wagyu short rib.”

“Excellent choices, Mademoiselle. And what will you have to eat?”

The older woman hummed, letting her eyes rake over the menu one last time before deciding, “I’d like the Sea Bass, please.”

“Certainly, I’ll be right back with your wine.” Pierre replied, glancing briefly at Vi and shooting the younger woman a wink before walking away.

Vi cleared her throat, “You ordered for me.”

Caitlyn lowered her now empty glass and frowned, “Sorry, did I take it too far? Would you like something else?”

The younger woman shook her head, “No, no. It was sexy. I especially liked the way you pronounced Bonneau,” she replied, butchering the word in an endearing way.

Caitlyn giggled, “I should hope so, I am fluent in French.”

Vi scoffed playfully, “Of course you are. Is there anything you can’t do?”

“Badminton.” The older woman deadpanned.

“Badminton?” Vi shot her a look of incredulity.

Caitlyn nodded, “I played for a bit when I was young and still living in Cambridge. I was absolute rubbish.”

She could run for hours through the fields beyond the Kiramman estate, sure-footed beneath open skies, but put a racquet in her hand and she became all elbows and tangled feet.  She hated every match—and she always lost.

“I’m sure you weren’t that bad,” Vi reassured.

Caitlyn shook her head, fiddling with the edges of the napkin in her lap, unable to make eye-contact, “It was utterly humiliating,” she admitted. “I never even wanted to play.”

Vi tilted her head, sensing there was more to the story, “So, why did you?”

The older woman cleared her throat, “My parents have always set high expectations of me,” she explained, “The Kiramman name means something in the upper echelons of society. Ever since I was a child, I was expected to live up to it.”

“I don’t get it, how exactly does playing badminton accomplish that?” Vi asked with a frown.

The pure look of confusion on Vi’s face made Caitlyn laugh, “Exactly, darling. It didn’t. They eventually pulled me from the sport after I tripped over my own foot and faceplanted into the court in the middle of a match.”

The younger woman laughed with her, though part of her felt guilty for doing so considering Caitlyn said the other kids made fun of her.  But the older woman didn’t show any sign of discomfort, thankfully.

There was curiosity there, and patience, she realized.  Vi wasn’t pushing, but she was showing Caitlyn that she was interested in knowing her.  Not just the surface-level stuff, but the things that have broken her down and built her up again.

Vi’s lips parted slightly, but she didn’t look away.  She just squeezed their joined hands once and took a sip of her wine.

“Everything about my life has been a series of decisions made for me, not by me.” Caitlyn said. “That is, everything before Virginia.”

“Will you tell me about it?” Vi didn’t miss a beat, just leaned in closer, her thumb rubbing absentminded circles against the older woman’s knuckles.

Caitlyn sighed, “I’m afraid it isn’t very interesting.”

“It is to me,” Vi countered.

Taking a sip of her wine, Caitlyn let its redolence coat her tongue, savouring the velvety feel of it before setting the glass down and catching Vi’s waiting eyes again, “Life in New York was…lonely. And intense. I was merely a teen when we moved to the city and let’s just say I didn’t fit in well with the American education system.”

She huffed a laugh, reminiscing about the days she spent getting lost in the high school hallways and the lunch breaks she spent eating her marmalade sandwiches alone in the stairwell.

“In a way I was better off because I was able to focus on my studies, which was exactly what my mother and father wanted. They always wanted me to go to law school and to follow in my mother’s footsteps.”

Vi tilted her head, “But it wasn’t what you wanted?”

It was more rhetorical than it was an actual question, but Caitlyn answered it regardless, “No, it wasn’t.” she agreed.

The younger woman hummed at that, “What would you have studied if the choice had been up to you?”

She didn’t even need to think about it, “Biology or Ecological studies. I probably would’ve ended up studying some rare species out west somewhere.”

“And you think its too late?”

Caitlyn paused mid-sip of her wine in ponderance, then shook her head, “It just wasn’t meant to be.”

Vi’s lips parted like she wanted to follow up, but she was interrupted by Pierre and the server arriving with their food.

“Here we have the Wagyu short rib, slow braised for forty-eight hours, plated in a summer vegetable succotash.” The server announced, placing the delicate plate in front of Vi with care.

Next to him, Pierre eyed Caitlyn with a twinkle in his eye, like he’d grown fond of her in the span of an hour, “Mademoiselle, the pan-seared Antarctic Sea Bass in a vegetable minestrone, perfumed with Garlic and Pernod.”

“Merci, Pierre. Ça a l'air délicieux.” Caitlyn responded in a soft, elegant French accent that made the host stop in his tracks.

Pierre gasped, “Ton accent est magnifique!”

Caitlyn chuckled, and when she turned back to Vi, the younger woman was looking at her wide-eyed, her fork hovering above her plate.

“What?” she asked.

Vi shrugged, “Nothing.” And went back to eating her entrée like a hound that’d been starved for two days.

Across the table, Vi simply looked at her with amusement, “That good, huh?” she laughed.

Caitlyn hid her face and mumbled, “Oh, how humiliating.”

Vi sighed as she felt the woman’s warm breath brush against her skin, followed by the tenderness of her soft lips.  She wanted to lean into it, wanted nothing more for the kiss to linger, but she also knew they couldn’t.  Not in public, not like this.

Caitlyn finally broke the silence, her voice a whisper, “Now it is.” She replied with timid grin.

The younger woman let out a breathless chuckle but didn’t make any effort to move back to her chair across the table.  Instead, Vi simply reached for her own plate and pulled it closer, scooping up the last bite of short rib.

“Taste this,” she said, holding the fork in one hand with her other hand beneath it so the none of the braising liquid would drip on Caitlyn’s silk dress.

The older woman quirked a brow but didn’t question the fact that this was the last bite of Vi’s entrée before leaning closer and opening her mouth just enough to close her lips around the tines of the fork.

Decadent short rib dissolved on her tongue, the rich combined flavors of the braising liquid and the summer vegetables did somersaults on her taste buds.  Simply delicious.

“That was…”

“Delicious?” Vi asked with a grin.

The older woman shook her head, cheeks flushed so pink it looked like she’d been sunburned, “Intimate.”

Vi hummed, “Don’t worry, no one was looking.”

Caitlyn chuckled, “I don’t think I would’ve cared if they were.”

“Would either of you like to order dessert?” Pierre asked, popping up seemingly out of thin air.

It startled Caitlyn slightly, and she let out a soft yelp, her hand coming up to her chest to settle her racing heart.

Vi snickered, rubbing soothing circles on a silk-laden thigh to calm her before turning to the man, “We’ll share a lemon tartlet, please Pierre.”

The man nodded, winked, and then walked away.

“Now look who’s taking charge,” Caitlyn giggled, pulling Vi’s attention back toward her.

“Can’t let you work too hard, princess.” The younger woman replied with a mischievous smirk.

Caitlyn rolled her eyes at the nickname, despite how fond she’d grown of it.  She inhaled a breath, her smile fading into something more like consideration, “So, what about you Violet? What’s your adoptive father like?”

Unfortunately for Caitlyn, Vi’s smile faded at the question and her hand retreated from her thigh.  She missed her warmth immediately, wanting nothing more to seek it out.  But the older woman respected the distance she put between them.

“Sorry, you don’t have to answer that.” Caitlyn apologized quickly.

Vi shook her head, picking at an invisible speck of lint on her suit lapel, “No, it’s okay. You’re just curious.”

She considered Caitlyn’s question, watching the concern soften those cerulean eyes.  She could’ve shrugged it off.  Vi had spent most of her life turning painful truths into jokes before anyone could look too closely.

Instead, she stared at her hands, “Vander was good to us,” she began quietly. “That’s first and foremost.”

“He was…intense. And I don’t really blame him for that. I mean, he took over my parents’ business after they died and then had two grieving kids to feed on top of it.” Vi swallowed. “But he also wasn’t this big, cuddly father-figure that a lot of people in the community make him out to be.”

Caitlyn didn’t say anything, giving the younger woman room to continue.

“He loved us, never made us feel unwanted, but love doesn’t make more hours in the day.” Vi rubbed her thumb across her palm. “He worked dawn until after dark most days, and he’d come home smelling like engine grease and beer, then tell us to go do our homework in our rooms.”

Vi looked up, expecting to find pity laced in the older woman’s features, but instead found something closer to admiration.

Eventually, she spoke again, “I spent most of my time taking care of Powder until she was old enough to take care of herself, and then I started helping at the business, and Vander put a lot of pressure on me to bring more money in.  So, that’s when I got my guide certification.”

Caitlyn reached for her hand, slowly enough to let Vi pull away if she wanted to.  She didn’t.

“You deserved to have someone take care of you the way you did your sister,” Caitlyn whispered.

Vi looked at her then, eyes bright with unshed tears.

Caitlyn held her gaze, “You still do.”

With her free hand, she reached up, the pad of her thumb wiping away the moisture from the corner of the younger woman’s eye.

Vi swallowed her emotions, softening into the woman’s touch.  She’d never had this before—someone that was on her side, that made her feel safe.  It was terrifying.

“Ahem,” Pierre cleared his throat, appearing once again out of the blue, this time with a small porcelain plate topped with a slice of lemon tart and blackberry frozen yogurt. 

The host set it down on the table in front of them and grinned, “Pour les formidables filles amoureuses, une tarte au citron fraîchement préparée par le chef.”

Caitlyn’s eyes widened and she felt heat fill her stomach at Pierre’s choice of words, “Filles amoureuses?”

The host chuckled, a deep and resonant sound that echoed across the tiled floor, “Oui, chéri. Vous deux êtes amoureux, n'est-ce pas?”

Vi watched Pierre and Caitlyn speak fluent French, probably something about the ingredients of the tart or maybe if the chef had grown the lemons fresh, she wasn’t sure.  All she knew was that it was making her head buzz.

Caitlyn hummed, “Pas encore, mais j'espère l'être.”

Whatever the older woman had said made Pierre’s smile turn affectionate.  The man considered them for a few beats before nodding and leaving them to eat their tart.

“What was that about?” Vi asked as she reached for a spoon.

Caitlyn bit her cheek, “Just asking about the lemons.”

Neither woman was particularly keen on leaving, so once they finished their dessert and expressed their gratitude to the staff that’d served them, they found themselves outside in the manicured garden behind the Inn.

Long rows of low boxwood hedges formed a symmetrical pattern of enclosed flower beds, which bordered several brick and pea-gravel walkways.  Ivy climbed wrought iron fences in the distance, and a fountain stood in the center circle of the garden, the water trickling softly in the quiet, nighttime breeze.

Fragrant rose bushes contrasted well with the earthy soil, mingling with the woody scent of the Inn’s chimney.  The crickets seemed to sing to them as they strolled under the warm glow of the garden lights, Caitlyn’s heels clacking a steady beat against the brick.

A slender hand rested comfortably in a strong, callused one, their fingers having found each other as soon as they’d stepped outside.

It was cooler than it had been when they arrived, since it was well past nine at night.  Caitlyn shivered as a gust blew past them.

Vi stopped walking and her hand slipped from Caitlyn’s grasp, which made the older woman turn toward her with a frown.

The younger woman chuckled, “Just wait a second,” she whispered, shaking her head in amusement as she slid her blazer off her strong arms, then stepped around the taller woman and placed it over her shoulders.

Caitlyn felt the immediate relief of Vi’s lingering body heat as the fabric sheltered her from the evening chill, sighing contentedly, “My knight is so chivalrous,” she joked.

Vi barked out a laugh, finding the woman’s slender hand again, “Only for you, princess.”

Despite the running joke of the night, those words felt dangerous to Caitlyn, settling somewhere deep beneath her ribs.

Yes, Jayce had warned her about Vi—about the women who drifted through her life and the walls she kept firmly between herself and anything resembling commitment.  But Vi had brought her here, somewhere precious, somewhere she had never shared with another woman.

The contradiction made Caitlyn’s chest ache.  She foolishly, desperately, wanted to believe that meant she might be different.

It’s not like she couldn’t just be straightforward and ask Vi.  They were alone.  They had the privacy of the garden with no one to bother them or see her inevitably cry when the younger woman told her she didn’t feel the same.

But like she’d decided earlier that day, Caitlyn didn’t want to ruin this night.  Especially considering this had been the best date she’d ever been on.

“You’re doing that thing where you think too hard again,” Vi’s voice broke her from whatever spell she’d fallen under. 

Caitlyn blinked and found the younger woman watching her intently.

“S-sorry, guess it’s a bit past my bedtime,” the older woman sighed.

Vi’s grey eyes narrowed, “That’s not it, though, is it, Caitlyn?”

It was the first time, aside from introducing her to Pierre earlier, that the younger woman used her full name all evening.

“No, but would it be so wrong if I asked that we just enjoy the evening without getting caught up in whatever my brain is doing?”

They came to a stop just in front of the fountain, the trickle of water a bit louder here than it had been on the outer edges of the garden.  Some of its misty overspray coated their skin, though they didn’t pay it any mind.

Vi slid her free hand up and cupped Caitlyn’s cheek, letting the warmth of her palm soothe her, “Your brain is a beautiful thing,” she whispered.

And before she could even argue, Vi was leaning in, closing the distance between them, slow enough that Caitlyn could pull away if she really wanted to.

But she didn’t.  She would never.

Their warm breaths mingled and the tips of their noses touched, and for a half-second, Caitlyn thought she was dreaming.  Or floating on a cloud somewhere.  Anything but standing here under a starlit sky by a trickling water fountain, about to kiss a woman she’d chosen of her own volition.

When their lips met, it was incredibly soft, and warm, and tasted sweet like the lingering citrus of the lemon tart they’d shared.  Vi’s breathing went shaky, and little puffs of warm air caressed Caitlyn’s cheek.

The younger woman’s hand slid down to her neck, pulling her closer as the chaste kiss deepened into something more reverent.  And all Caitlyn could do was melt into it, wrapping her arms around Vi’s neck and kissing her back.

Either this was the start of something that’d be a part of them forever, or this would be their downfall.  But in that moment, none of it mattered.  Only the way each other’s lips felt against their own.

Vi thought there was nothing worse than the sight of Caitlyn’s frown, so, leaving their fairytale date took a lot more convincing than she’d originally guessed would be necessary.  Eventually, however, she’d managed to escort the older woman to the car and drive her home, later than she probably would’ve liked, but she too couldn’t be bothered to end the date any more than Caitlyn could’ve.

All good things must come to an end, however, and that’s why they found themselves standing beneath the dim light on Jayce’s front porch, its bulb flickering each time a moth flew into it.

The windows were all dark, a telltale sign that despite his best efforts to wait up for them, the Chief Ranger had gone to bed.

Caitlyn’s perfect updo had fallen out slightly, a few strands of midnight-blue framed her face, and her lipstick had smudged, a fact that Vi was certainly smug about.  Even as exhaustion laced her features after a very long twenty-four hours, Vi thought the older woman looked as radiant as ever.

“Would you care to come in for a spot of tea?” Caitlyn asked, voice sleepy.

Vi wanted nothing more than to follow Caitlyn through that door.  But she also knew the implications of that question, and if she was going to treat the older woman as something different than all the women she’d lay with before, the first step on that list was to respect her enough to not sleep with her on the first date.

She sighed, “Not tonight. It’s getting late, Princess.”

Caitlyn, much to Vi’s surprise, didn’t look upset by her answer.  In fact, she actually seemed…relieved, her grin widening like she had made the right choice. 

The taller woman leaned over and pressed her lips to Vi’s cheek, “Good, that was a trick question.” She whispered, the warmth of her breath dusting the younger woman’s ear.

When Caitlyn returned to her full height, she was smirking devilishly, a mischievous twinkle in her cerulean eyes.  She pulled her bottom lip between her teeth before speaking again, “Goodnight, Violet.”

“Goodnight, Miss Kiramman,” Vi replied, struck still where she stood, staring at the wooden door long after Caitlyn disappeared behind it.

Y'all have no idea how excited I was to write this specific chapter. I've been working on this since even before chapter 6 was posted because I knew I wanted this scene to be perfect and I hope my ideas and the setting translated well.

let me know what your favorite parts were in the comments below, and as always, hit that kudos button if you're enjoying the story so far <3

***French translations (I had help from my mom who is not fluent ok so these might not be perfect):

1. Merci, Pierre. Ça a l'air délicieux. - "Thank you, Pierre. This looks delicious."
2. Ton accent est magnifique! - "Your accent is magnificent!"
3. Pour les formidables filles amoureuses, une tarte au citron fraîchement préparée par le chef. - "For the lover girls, a lemon tart prepared fresh by the chef."
4. Filles amoureuses - lover girls
5. Oui, chéri. Vous deux êtes amoureux, n'est-ce pas - "Yes, darling. You're both in love, are you not?"
6. Pas encore, mais j'espère l'être. - "Not yet, but I hope to be."
